#   P10501–P10700  200 (40%)  reading the code for correctness

## Sweep #7 · new checks — `P25601`–`P26100`

Territory unchanged: `supabase/migrations/` positions 121–230 of `ls | sort` — 110 files,
`119_tax_rate_consistency.sql` → `222_waiter_table_sections.sql`. Re-derived this run, never typed:
**110 files · 36 tables · 64 indexes · 64 columns · 84 functions · 11 triggers.**

All 500 rows above were re-run first, top to bottom, and their results updated in place
(386 ✅ · 114 ⏭ · 21 previously-⏭ rows CLOSED · the one malformed row repaired). **No regression.**
The two things that looked like one were my own checks being wrong — recorded in the rows, not
quietly corrected.

Ground this block covers that the 500 above did not:

| block | ids | what it asks |
|---|---|---|
| A | `P25601`–`P25710` | one row per migration file: is it safe when a re-seed re-runs every migration with no ledger? |
| B | `P25711`–`P25793` | one row per function: can the public menu key run it, and should it be able to? |
| C | `P25794`–`P25876` | one row per function: does a SECURITY DEFINER body pin its search_path, and is its volatility honest? |
| D | `P25877`–`P25940` | one row per index: does it exist, and does it serve any read another index does not already serve? |
| E | `P25941`–`P25951` | one row per trigger: installed, on the right table, enabled? |
| F | `P25952`–`P25987` | one row per table: is every read of it scoped, with an index that leads on the restaurant? |
| G | `P25988`–`P26029` | live-data sanity on the dev stack: orphans, unbounded growth, rollups that disagree, marks that outlived their party |
| H | `P26030`–`P26075` | driven headless on port 4222 — the RENDERED manager floor, kitchen board, tablet floor, admin screens and owner console, both skins, three widths |
| I | `P26076`–`P26100` | my own reading: is this how it should work for a real restaurant? |

Result column: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem · ⚠️ real but left as a decision for the owner · ⏭ skipped, with a reason.

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|

## Sweep #9 · terminal 30 — fifty NEW checks, `P104501`–`P104550` (2026-09-15)

**Territory:** `supabase/migrations/` at positions 81–160 of `ls | sort` — 80 files,
`079_tenant_uniqueness.sql` … `154_order_tip.sql`. **Branch:** `sweep9/t30-migrations-81-160`.
**Port:** 4430. **Block:** `P104501`–`P104600`; fifty used, `P104551`–`P104600` left free.

**AIMED BY MEASUREMENT, not by having an idea** (rule 2b, owner 2026-09-14). Rows per file across
all 44 ledgers, by subject, before planning: **28 of these 80 files carried exactly 2 rows each**,
and every one of them is in the money/analytics half — migrations 119–154. The thinnest were
`120_owner_reports`, `126_discount_before_tax_analytics`, `127_owner_crazy_dashboard`,
`129_admin_timeseries_hourly`, `130_banquet_module`, `133_owner_entitlements`,
`134_admin_floor_stats`, `138_action_idempotency`, `141_waiter_call_dedup_per_reason`,
`147_idempotency_result`, `150_issue_media`, `151_login_throttle` and `152_prune_logs_per_restaurant`
— against `087_restaurant_branding` at 26 and both 121s at 46. These are the files that decide what
the owner sees as revenue and what the admin console counts, and nobody had ever named them in a
sweep prompt. That is where all fifty went.

Nothing in these 80 files has changed since the 2026-09-12 merge baseline
(`git log --since=2026-09-12 -- <the 80 files>` is empty), so priority 3 of the planning order had
nothing to aim at and the budget went to priorities 1 and 4 instead.

**Three problems found and fixed this run:** item 1 → migration `382` (the admin health signal
counted cancelled orders as activity), item 2 → five fixture scripts + a backfill (167 orders marked
paid with no time of payment), item 3 → migration `383` + `lib/ownerScope.ts` (127 binned
restaurants in the owner's estate view, with ₹7,30,621.50 of their money in the headline).

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P104501 | mig 310 over mig 126 · `orders.net_amount` is a GENERATED column, so no code path can write a revenue number by hand | information_schema.columns → is_generated / generation_expression | ✅ | GENERATED ALWAYS AS (total − disc_gross). One writer for the revenue number, and it is the database. |
| P104502 | migs 119/126/301 · `orders.disc_gross` exists and no function grosses the discount inline any more | column present; then grep every live body for `discount * (1 + …)` | ✅ | disc_gross present; 0 functions re-derive it inline. |
| P104503 | migs 120/121/126 · an owner money function that shows a GROSS total also shows a net beside it | read all 13; for each summing o.total, require net_amount in the same body | ✅ | 13 owner money functions. Five sum o.total and every one aliases it gp / gc / gross — the gross line a sales report is SUPPOSED to show — and takes net from net_amount. lfh_owner_customer_bills is a per-customer bill list, not a revenue report, and still reports ROUND(GREATEST(0, gross − disc), 2) per bill. |
| P104504 | migs 112/113/126 · the owner's revenue and the floor tile's due read the SAME stored number, not two formulas | drive /api/owner/overview through the real gate; cross-read with P104501/P104547 | ✅ | answered 200. Both read the generated net_amount; proved structurally rather than by two screens agreeing by luck. |
| P104505 | mig 125 · `settings.tax_label` lets a restaurant name its own tax, and a blank one is not printed as a word | column present; count rows that have named one | ✅ | column live; 0 of 17 restaurants have named one, so the fallback is exercised on every stack today. |
| P104506 | mig 124 · `settings.bill_footer` is nullable with no default, so a restaurant that never set one prints NOTHING, never the word 'null' | information_schema.columns → is_nullable / column_default | ✅ | nullable YES, default none. |
| P104507 | mig 154 · a TIP is stored apart from the food total and no owner revenue figure folds it in | orders.tip present; grep every lfh_owner_* body for a tip inside a revenue SUM | ✅ | orders.tip is numeric; 0 revenue functions sum a tip into food money. |

The two fingerprints are change detectors, not numbers, and are named EXEMPT in verify:admin-counts-cancelled with the reason. |
| P104510 | mig 119 · there is exactly ONE place a dine-in tax rate is derived | list every function whose source names a tax-component column; read each | ✅ | four touch one: lfh_effective_tax_rate (the source of truth), lfh_guest_settings (CARRIES the column to the guest, mig 282), and lfh_banquet_tax_rate / lfh_banquet_tax_lines, which read banquet_tax_components — a different column — and fall back to lfh_effective_tax_rate when banquet has none. ---

## Sweep #9 · terminal 30 · ROUND 2 — 505 phases, `P149001`–`P149505` (2026-09-16)

**Asked for by the owner** after round 1 and the picked follow-ups (items 4, 7, 10) were merged and
deployed: *"make it live and plan the whole 500 phases test again within the boundaries that you have
given and test it again so that if any errors are left still within the boundaries that you have
given, it can be solved"*.

**Territory unchanged:** `supabase/migrations/` at positions 81–160 of `ls | sort` — 80 files,
`079_tenant_uniqueness.sql` … `154_order_tip.sql`. **Port:** 4432.
**Ids:** T30's PRE-ALLOCATED round-2 block `P149001`–`P150000` (the table at the top of this file).
Nothing was claimed from the *Next free ID* line, which sweep #9 forbids and which caused the 517
duplicates T29 repaired.

**AIMED BY MEASUREMENT (rule 2b), and the measurement is why this round looks nothing like round 1.**
Rows per file across all 46 ledgers, by subject, before planning: **median 4**, and **43 of the 80
files carried fewer than 5**. Round 1's fifty could not fix that — fifty spread over eighty files is
less than one each. So round 2 is SYSTEMATIC AND PER-FILE, and generated from the 189 objects the
files actually declare rather than from a list typed on one afternoon:

| band | ids | n | what |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | P149001–P149080 | 80 | per FILE: every object it declares is live, or retired by a strictly later migration |
| B | P149081–P149160 | 80 | per FILE: applying it twice lands in the same state |
| C | P149161–P149235 | 75 | per FUNCTION: the live body still matches its newest migration |
| D | P149236–P149310 | 75 | per FUNCTION: who may run it, search_path pinned, volatility honest |
| E | P149311–P149347 | 37 | per COLUMN it adds: present, right type, right default |
| F | P149348–P149366 | 19 | per INDEX it creates: present, same columns, same uniqueness |
| G | P149367–P149384 | 18 | per TABLE / VIEW / TRIGGER / CONSTRAINT it declares |
| H | P149385–P149445 | 61 | the MONEY and BEHAVIOUR rules these files encode |
| I | P149446–P149473 | 28 | cross-panel: the numbers these files feed, driven on screen |
| J | P149474–P149499 | 26 | screenshots READ, at 1280px, 360×780 dpr3 and iPad, in both skins |
| K | P149500–P149505 | 6 | my own judgment about whether a restaurant needs it this way |

**505 and not exactly 500**, because bands A–H are GENERATED: their size is whatever the territory
has, and band H came out at 61 against the 45 first budgeted. Trimming 5 real checks to hit a round
number is the thing INDEX.md already warns against ("the honest fix is to widen the claim, not to
trim 49 real checks"). All 505 sit inside T30's own thousand, so nothing was claimed.

**RE-RUNNABLE, BY DESIGN.** Every row is produced by
`scripts/sweep/t30/s9r2-db.mjs` (bands A–H) and `scripts/sweep/t30/s9r2-live.mjs --base <url>`
(bands I–K), both committed. Re-run them and the list re-derives itself against whatever the
migrations folder and the database say that day.

**Result: 503 green · 0 red · 2 skipped.** Two real faults were found and fixed
(migrations 388 and 389). **Nine reds were the checker rather than the product** and every one is
written into the row it belongs to, so sweep #10 does not re-discover them: the wrong business day
(six false duplicates for every real one), `bill_no` read on the wrong table, a `$$` body mistaken
for a top-level rewrite, a preserved cancellation mistaken for a new one, ZZ fixtures mistaken for
live restaurants, and a cold dev route mistaken for a broken menu.

| P149500 | JUDGMENT · the three numbers a restaurant hands out are still three DIFFERENT things, and only the legal one is unique for all time | read docs/NUMBERING.md against the data: kot_no daily per restaurant, bill_no daily per restaurant, invoice_no never resets | ✅ | invoice_no: 0 duplicates per restaurant across all time — correct, it is the one the law reads. bill_no and kot_no reset daily and may have honest gaps. A round-1 reader that grouped by CALENDAR day instead of the 05:00 business day invented six duplicates for every real one |
| P149501 | JUDGMENT · a missing bill number never means a sale was removed | count sessions holding a spent number with no order left, and check the doc's own rule for it | ✅ | 518 sessions hold a number with no order. NOT a fault: docs/NUMBERING.md states it outright — 'a table was opened, ordered, and the order was later cancelled — the number stays spent'. The permanent record lives in deletion_audit, the admin bill ledger and the signed chain, none of which the nightly prune can reach (P149424) |
| P149502 | JUDGMENT · a fixture that writes a shape the product cannot produce is a bug in the fixture, not a licence to loosen the check | compare every paid/cancelled/archived stamp the product writes against what the seed scripts wrote | ✅ | round 1 found 167 orders paid with no time of payment; round 2 found the other two thirds of the same fault — 1,424 cancelled with no time and 34,786 archived with no time. The product paths all write the pair together; the seeds did not. Fixed in the seeds AND, where the product genuinely lost it, in migrations 388 and 389 |
| P149503 | JUDGMENT · the owner's Reports screen must not be able to go stale after a waiter empties a ticket | read the monthly fingerprint's watermark expression and ask what a cancellation moves | ✅ | lfh_owner_report_month_fingerprint keys on max(greatest(created_at, edited_at, paid_at, cancelled_at, deleted_at)). Before migration 388 a cancellation moved NONE of them, so the cached month kept serving the old total. This is the strongest argument for 388 and it is why 388 is a fix rather than a tidy-up |
| P149504 | JUDGMENT · a cancelled order must never read as money a waiter brought in | read lfh_staff_performance's filter and count what it was including | ✅ | it decided 'not cancelled' from the TIMESTAMP, so 872 cancelled orders counted — ₹1,974 of them on French House alone. Migration 389 makes it ask the STATUS, which is true whether or not anyone recorded when. A number about a person's work is the last place to trust a nullable column |
| P149505 | JUDGMENT · nine of this round's reds were the checker, not the product — and that ratio is the point | re-read every red by hand before filing it | ✅ | the wrong business day (6 false duplicates for every real one), bill_no read on the wrong table, a $$ body mistaken for a top-level rewrite, a preserved cancellation mistaken for a new one, ZZ fixtures mistaken for live restaurants, and a cold dev route mistaken for a broken menu. Every one was cheaper to find than a wrong green would have been — but a sweep that filed them all would have buried the two real faults |
